# MDSE Rule-to-Model Traceability Matrix

**Updated 2026-10-25.** 386 original rules; 727 derived concept/gap rows. All data is trace evidence, not a statement of approved SysML semantics.

**Reading:** Each row shows its source rule, the derived element in its own type column, and the **first available** existing candidate for Need, Use Case, Function and Object (name and statement). Where further candidates exist, use the linked [decomposition register](71_Rule_Analysis/DECOMPOSITION_REGISTER.csv) and the individual source/analysis notes. The original rule text appears on its first row; subsequent rows link its rule note. Rule→model candidate links are **not validated native relationships**. Use-case→need links reflect the existing native UC note, but upstream rule→UC is still provisional.

**GOV-001 owner decision:** Proposed customer need *Adaptable Tool* → use case *Integrate an Approved Software Change* → function *Manage Software Change*. These are approved modeling directions, not yet created native element notes.

**Sources:** [RULESET](https://github.com/spencerskelly/MDSE_v2/blob/main/governance/RULESET.md) · [rules](70_Rule_References/RULE_TRACE_REGISTER.csv) · [decomposition](71_Rule_Analysis/DECOMPOSITION_REGISTER.csv) · [requirements](72_Derived_Requirements/REQUIREMENT_CANDIDATES.csv) · [verification](73_Derived_Verification/VERIFICATION_CANDIDATES.csv).

| [GOV-011](70_Rule_References/GOV/GOV-001_020/GOV-011.md) — Proposed · Standing — One coordinated release manifest pins compatible Base Vault, Importer, Bootstrap, Workbench and SysML rule/schema versions and hashes. Base owns default assets and managed settings; Bootstrap owns installation and upgrades; Workbench owns interactive model writes; Importer owns import reconciliation. No tool overwrites another tool's user-owned data. Author identity is provenance, not authentication; credentials stay in OS or service stores, never shared YAML. (REG-040, V2-58 (Shared Note and Release Contract)) | [STK-00013 Engineering Knowledge Base](10_Stakeholder_Needs/Engineering%20Knowledge%20Base.md) — Shared product knowledge, structure, specifications and relationships. | [UC-00001 Import an Engineering Model](20_Use_Cases/Import%20an%20Engineering%20Model.md) — A user imports engineering knowledge from multiple source formats, retaining provenance and reviewable disposition of every processed source record. | — | [BEH-00002 Ingest and Reconcile Engineering Sources](30_Functions/Ingest%20and%20Reconcile%20Engineering%20Sources.md) — Import source data with adapters, identity, reconciliation and dispositions. | [OBJ-00002 MDSE Core](50_Architecture/MDSE%20Core/MDSE%20Core.md) — Shared model parsing, validation, serialization and identity. | — | Supporting; trace unvalidated |
| [GOV-012](70_Rule_References/GOV/GOV-001_020/GOV-012.md) — Active · Standing — Tool ownership: Workbench owns UI behavior, disposable index, ModelRef navigation, schema-valid edit transactions and Review disposition (not semantics, importer mapping or release packaging). Bootstrap owns safe activation repair, release verification and author registration. Importer owns source translation, naming/identity allocation and import evidence (not editing or plugin management). Base builder owns the runtime payload, governed plugin config and pins/hashes. Cross-vault is deferred (archived resolver is reference only). A tool may not silently broaden or redefine model semantics; archived material never overrides a current source. | [MOD-012](70_Rule_References/MOD/MOD-001_020/MOD-012.md) — Active · Standing — One global 30-character identity-token namespace spans notes and Local Model records; a token is never reused. | [MOD-075](70_Rule_References/MOD/MOD-061_080/MOD-075.md) — Active · Standing · live — For a SysML v2 relationship, legal endpoints and ownership come from the native SysML construct and its typed model, not from `relationships.yaml`; the authoritative `## SysML v2` block is validated independently. Until OD-01 completes the field-by-field split, V1 entries in that schema do not authorize, prohibit or duplicate native SysML relationships. | [MOD-076](70_Rule_References/MOD/MOD-061_080/MOD-076.md) — Active · Standing · live — For native SysML v2 relationships, Workbench and importer read the forward meaning from the authoritative `## SysML v2` block and derive reverse navigation from it; no SysML-defined inverse is persisted in YAML. For a relationship approved as MDSE-only YAML under OD-01, any stored inverse is a non-authoritative derived field: the forward value wins on disagreement. The translator and Workbench, not Nodian, are the only writers of such MDSE-only inverses. This does not approve the still-legacy schema entries before OD-01 classification. | [MOD-136](70_Rule_References/MOD/MOD-121_140/MOD-136.md) — Unreviewed · P1 — Change handling: unchanged inputs produce zero edits; repeated runs are idempotent; full rebuild is recovery only. Stage edits, validate, report conflicts, then write only changed files while keeping unrelated notes and human content. Imported deletions get a tombstone/review and never silently erase manually edited notes or unmodeled attachments. Keep an undo manifest of pre-change hashes. Updates are staged for diff/PR approval and never overwrite another contributor's work. | [MOD-165](70_Rule_References/MOD/MOD-161_180/MOD-165.md) — Active · P1 · live — The SysML v2 language is documented one note per construct (element and relationship) in `governance/language/`, built from `catalog.json`. Each note gives the description, the elements it can come from and go to, what separates it from the most similar construct, an example, and how often engineers use it (Everyday, Common, Occasional, Rare). The notes ship in every vault under `99_System/04_Language`. Entries are grouped as language constructs, standard-library concepts and modeling patterns, and each note states separately whether the construct is documented, recognized by the scanner, written by the importer, and accepted by a SysML v2 parser; a listed construct is not claimed as supported. | [IMP-091](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-081_100/IMP-091.md) — Unreviewed · P1 — Connector family mapping (26 families, 14,688 connectors): Generalization to specialization; Abstraction:allocate to allocation; Aggregation to owned PartUsage where physical containment is evidenced, else review; Dependency:satisfy to satisfy; Realisation to perform/allocate by endpoint kinds, else review; refine to Dependency + Refinement metadata; Association to ConnectionUsage only if connectivity is proven; UseCase:extend to optional case usage; UseCase:include to required nested case usage; ControlFlow to Succession with guards; StateFlow to transition; Nesting to approved nested usage else organizational; NoteLink to YAML links only; Sequence reference-only; Usage to generic Dependency; Dependency:verify to verification; Connector to port-to-port ConnectionUsage; Realisation:deriveReqt takes precedence over generic Realisation. Eligibility is per connector, never per family. | [IMP-096](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-081_100/IMP-096.md) — Active · P1 — Structure, links, quantities and multiplicity are defined by the SysML v2 block and are not repeated in `## Source: EA`. Relationships SysML v2 has no form for, and identifying properties, stay in the YAML (`describes`, `eaType`, `uid`, `id`). `## Source: EA` holds only what is stored nowhere else: the note's EA GUID, the original name when the file name differs, alias, the kept tags, trigger and guard text, unresolved connectors and attachments. EA author, created date, type, stereotype and package are not written: the uid carries date and author, `eaType` carries type and stereotype, the folder carries the package. | [IMP-101](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-101.md) — Active · P1 — An EA Port becomes a port usage written `port name : [~]PortDef[mult]`. The type is the Port's PDATA1 (an EA interface Class, which is a port definition, IMP-104); the Classifier field is not the type. The multiplicity is the Multiplicity field, read as for parts; `~` is written when the object has an `isConjugated` tag with a true value (`true`, `yes`, `1` or EA's `-1`). A Port whose PDATA1 is not a port definition (a connector, a signal) is written without a type, the target is kept as a `- Port &lt;name&gt; is typed by [[…]]` line in Source, and the usage is flagged (`UNRESOLVED_PORT_TYPE:TARGET_IS_…`). | [IMP-102](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-102.md) — Active · P1 — A file or folder name is made safe for the browser's folder writer and for Windows: no control, bidi, line-separator or special characters, no lone surrogates, no trailing dot or space, and a device name (Con, Aux, Nul, Prn, Com0-9, Lpt0-9) is reserved with any extension, so `Con.pdf` becomes `Con_.pdf`. The page checks every path before writing anything and lists any that would fail; a write error names its path. | [IMP-103](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-103.md) — Active · P1 — No folder or file name is cut to fit a path length; the 400-character path limit is handled after the import, not by the importer. A name is changed only for the safety rules of IMP-102 (the 150-character part limit, which is marked). The folder write retries a file up to six times with a growing delay on InvalidStateError, NoModificationAllowedError or NotReadableError (antivirus, cloud sync or the indexer touching a new file), and a write error names the browser error, its message and the path. | [IMP-106](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-106.md) — Active · P1 — A Port under a Part is the Part's block's own port and is not written as a usage of the Part. It merges, with one hop and never up the redefinition chain: (1) PDATA3 names a Port owned by the Part's block; (2) else the block has exactly one port with the same non-blank name; (3) else it is added to the block as a port of its own, flagged for review, and same-named ones on one block combine. A Part with no block defers the Port (`USAGE_WITHOUT_NOTE_OWNER`). A merged Port types an untyped block port from its PDATA1. | [IMP-113](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-113.md) — Active · P1 — An Aggregation or Composition makes the child a usage in the whole, of the kind its two definitions are: part/part is `part`, action/action `action`, state/state `state`, item/item `item`, requirement/requirement `requirement`, part/port def `port`, use case/use case `use case`. The whole is the end marked composite or shared. Every connector is its own usage, even with the same two ends; it takes the child end's role name, else the child's name with `_2` suffixes, and the child end's multiplicity (none is invented). An EA Part or nested element of that type is matched to one connector and not repeated. A connector whose whole and part sit in one cycle of ownership is left out as OWNERSHIP_CYCLE. A part that is the child of an interface is AGGREGATION_PART_IN_INTERFACE (review). (Test_Vault_ approved aggregation docs 2026-10-17; D-0038) | [STK-00013 Engineering Knowledge Base](10_Stakeholder_Needs/Engineering%20Knowledge%20Base.md) — Shared product knowledge, structure, specifications and relationships. | [UC-00001 Import an Engineering Model](20_Use_Cases/Import%20an%20Engineering%20Model.md) — A user imports engineering knowledge from multiple source formats, retaining provenance and reviewable disposition of every processed source record. | [REQ-31192](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31181_31200/REQ-31192%20-%20IMP-113%20Statement%201.md) — "An Aggregation or Composition makes the child a usage in the whole, of the kind its two definitions are: part/part is `part`, action/action `action`, state/state `state`, item/item `item`, requirement/requirement `requirement`, part/port def `port`, use case/use case `use case`." | [BEH-00002 Ingest and Reconcile Engineering Sources](30_Functions/Ingest%20and%20Reconcile%20Engineering%20Sources.md) — Import source data with adapters, identity, reconciliation and dispositions. | [OBJ-00001 MDSE Importer](50_Architecture/MDSE%20Importer/MDSE%20Importer.md) — Reads sources and produces reconciled model notes. | — | Candidate; trace unvalidated |

| [IMP-115](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-115.md) — Active · P1 · live — When an EA relationship involves an MDSE-only note (Issue, Change, Info, Artifact, Decision), emit a supported descriptive YAML relation only when source meaning is established: `describes` for a describing Info/Artifact/Document, `affects` for an Issue with supported target semantics, `dependsOn` for compatible Dependency or Usage, and MDSE-only `hasChild` for an approved non-SysML note aggregation. Otherwise preserve each connector as a `flagLink` in the owning or holding note and a `deferred_review` ledger row, keeping connector GUID, family, source/target identities and reason `PROVISIONAL_LINK`. Do not create new `tracesTo` YAML links, assign a `native` disposition, or infer a SysML relationship. Pre-existing `tracesTo` values are historical input requiring explicit later reconciliation, not authority for new writes. Source: EA preserves a review line for deferred connectors; approved YAML relations are not repeated there. (REG-291, REG-293, W-288; D-0010, D-0038, D-0069, D-0070) | [STK-00013 Engineering Knowledge Base](10_Stakeholder_Needs/Engineering%20Knowledge%20Base.md) — Shared product knowledge, structure, specifications and relationships. | [UC-00001 Import an Engineering Model](20_Use_Cases/Import%20an%20Engineering%20Model.md) — A user imports engineering knowledge from multiple source formats, retaining provenance and reviewable disposition of every processed source record. | [REQ-31199](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31181_31200/REQ-31199%20-%20IMP-115%20Statement%201.md) — "A relationship end that is an MDSE note (Issue, Change, Info, Artifact, Decision) has no SysML form and becomes a YAML link on the source note: `describes` when the note is Info, Artifact or Document, `dependsOn` for Dependency and Usage, `hasChild` for Aggregation, and otherwise the provisional `tracesTo` with PROVISIONAL_LINK." | [BEH-00002 Ingest and Reconcile Engineering Sources](30_Functions/Ingest%20and%20Reconcile%20Engineering%20Sources.md) — Import source data with adapters, identity, reconciliation and dispositions. | [OBJ-00001 MDSE Importer](50_Architecture/MDSE%20Importer/MDSE%20Importer.md) — Reads sources and produces reconciled model notes. | — | Candidate; trace unvalidated |

| [IMP-116](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-116.md) — Active · P1 · live — A ControlFlow between action usages or a StateFlow between state usages in one known owning definition is a native SysML v2 succession or transition in that owner (IMP-098), retaining any explicit trigger, guard and effect. If endpoints do not share a valid known owner and no native relationship can be emitted without inventing ownership, preserve the connector GUID, source/target and original trigger/guard/effect as Source: EA evidence plus a `flagLink` and `deferred_review` ledger row with `FLOW_NO_COMMON_OWNER` or `FLOW_ENDPOINT_NOT_USAGE`; **do not write a YAML `precedes` edge** for temporal SysML semantics. Existing YAML `precedes` links are historical data pending OD-01 and are not bulk-rewritten. | [IMP-124](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-121_140/IMP-124.md) — Active · P1 — A usage or definition name keeps what makes the EA name different. A voltage puts its V at the decimal point and takes its sign as a letter: `+3.3VDC` is `P3V3DC`, `-13.3VDC` is `N13V3DC`, `12.5V` is `12V5`, `12VDC` stays `12VDC`; an unsigned voltage that starts the name takes `P`, because an identifier cannot start with a digit (`3.3VDC` is `P3V3DC`, so it and `+3.3VDC` meet and the second is told apart by its type). Elsewhere a leading `+` is `plus`, a leading `-` is `minus`, a point between digits is `p`, `&` is `And`, `%` is `Pct`, `#` is `No`. A name that is only a number is named after its type (`wire1`, not `_1`). Where the identifier is not just a respelling of the EA name, the EA name is recorded in `## Source: EA`. Two usages with one name take the name of their type to tell them apart (`a`, then `a_IConductor`); only a repeat of the same type takes `_2`. Quoted SysML names are not used, because every Workbench reader would need to change. Definitions keep the full EA title as their name. (D-0041, D-0042; amends IMP-095, IMP-112) | [STK-00013 Engineering Knowledge Base](10_Stakeholder_Needs/Engineering%20Knowledge%20Base.md) — Shared product knowledge, structure, specifications and relationships. | [UC-00001 Import an Engineering Model](20_Use_Cases/Import%20an%20Engineering%20Model.md) — A user imports engineering knowledge from multiple source formats, retaining provenance and reviewable disposition of every processed source record. | [REQ-31215](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31201_31220/REQ-31215%20-%20IMP-124%20Statement%201.md) — "A usage or definition name keeps what makes the EA name different. | [IMP-125](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-121_140/IMP-125.md) — Active · P1 — The importer can check a run before it writes: the full translation runs in memory and lists every flag, every deferred row and every problem, with a verdict. BLOCKED: the translation failed, a BOM error, or a file name the browser or Windows refuses. REVIEW: rows that would be skipped, conflicts with edits in the vault, BOM parts that differ from the vault, more than 10% of elements deferred, or over 50 BOM warnings. FLAGS: only flags. CLEAN: none. The check also says what becomes of every source row: standard; merged; imported but a value did not follow the standard (counted by code; nothing lost, something guessed); not in the model and may be lost (deferred or skipped by an error); text only; excluded by policy. It names the EA tables that are never read. | [IMP-126](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-121_140/IMP-126.md) — Active · P1 — An import into an existing vault shows the MDSE release, schema and every shipped plugin in the vault against the ones in the importer, with a state for each (same, newer in the importer, vault is newer, not installed, same version with different files). Updating the toolset during the import is a choice: ticked by default only when the importer is newer, unticked when the vault is newer, forced on for a new vault. Unticked, the model is imported and the installed tools stay as they are. Ticked, it applies the same update as the maintenance section, with replaced files saved first. | [BOM-001](70_Rule_References/BOM/BOM-001_020/BOM-001.md) — Active · P2 — One reusable note per internal part number; revisions and occurrences never duplicate it. The parent assembly note owns its BOM usages with counts, quantities and applicability; a child note holds no data about the assemblies above it, and where-used is a reverse query, never written into the child. Separate discrete multiplicity from measured quantity and unit; preserve raw values; never guess units or double count. (REG-311, V2-22 (BOM Integration), D-0063) *Note: Owner-approved 2026-10-24 after removing the parent references.* | [STK-00013 Engineering Knowledge Base](10_Stakeholder_Needs/Engineering%20Knowledge%20Base.md) — Shared product knowledge, structure, specifications and relationships. | [UC-00001 Import an Engineering Model](20_Use_Cases/Import%20an%20Engineering%20Model.md) — A user imports engineering knowledge from multiple source formats, retaining provenance and reviewable disposition of every processed source record. | — | [BEH-00002 Ingest and Reconcile Engineering Sources](30_Functions/Ingest%20and%20Reconcile%20Engineering%20Sources.md) — Import source data with adapters, identity, reconciliation and dispositions. | [OBJ-00001 MDSE Importer](50_Architecture/MDSE%20Importer/MDSE%20Importer.md) — Reads sources and produces reconciled model notes. | — | Candidate; trace unvalidated |
| [BOM-002](70_Rule_References/BOM/BOM-001_020/BOM-002.md) — Unreviewed · P2 — One effective BOM per parent and configuration/revision. Competing simultaneous effective BOMs block automatic interpretation. Historical revisions stay in the source ledger. Family similarity is not automatically specialization, variation or substitutability. (REG-312, V2-23 (BOM Integration)) *Note: Proposed.* | [STK-00013 Engineering Knowledge Base](10_Stakeholder_Needs/Engineering%20Knowledge%20Base.md) — Shared product knowledge, structure, specifications and relationships. | [UC-00001 Import an Engineering Model](20_Use_Cases/Import%20an%20Engineering%20Model.md) — A user imports engineering knowledge from multiple source formats, retaining provenance and reviewable disposition of every processed source record. | — | [BEH-00002 Ingest and Reconcile Engineering Sources](30_Functions/Ingest%20and%20Reconcile%20Engineering%20Sources.md) — Import source data with adapters, identity, reconciliation and dispositions. | [OBJ-00001 MDSE Importer](50_Architecture/MDSE%20Importer/MDSE%20Importer.md) — Reads sources and produces reconciled model notes. | — | Candidate; trace unvalidated |
| [BOM-003](70_Rule_References/BOM/BOM-001_020/BOM-003.md) — Active · P2 — A row with blank internal Part Number and populated Manufacturing Part Number is an alternate of the preceding primary line in the same parent, revision, configuration and BOM group. Consecutive rows attach to the same primary. It is not a child usage and adds no quantity. With no unambiguous preceding primary it is review-only. Acceptance: the three-line example yields 2 required PC-100 occurrences. (REG-313, V2-24 (BOM-ALT-001)) A row on line 1xx (101 to 199) whose line xx exists under the same parent and revision is also an alternate of line xx, even when it has its own part number: it is listed under that line in the Source section with its part number, name, quantity and row, adds no quantity, creates no usage or note, and raises `ALT_BY_LINE` for review. | [BOM-015](70_Rule_References/BOM/BOM-001_020/BOM-015.md) — Active · P2 — The importer reads the BOM from `.xlsx`, `.csv` or `.tsv` in the page. Two layouts are recognised by header names: flat (Parent Part Number, Child Part Number, Linenumber, Amount, Unit …) and redline (Number, CL1, CL2, level columns 0..7 or Structure). Part numbers are read as text. In a redline, unstruck text in a cell is current and struck text is the previous value; a part number struck with no replacement is a removed line. A sheet with no recognised header is refused with the reason. The redline layout was checked against the four real redlines (D-0040): the header may sit below a preamble, the level may be an unlabelled column just left of the part number, the number column may be `Part Number`, and the unit may be `Unit`, `UOM` or `Unit Of Measure` (when `Unit Of Measure` exists, `Unit` is the quantity). | [BOM-016](70_Rule_References/BOM/BOM-001_020/BOM-016.md) — Active · P2 · live — Each part number gets one `Object` (subtype part) note named `&lt;PN&gt;` (the part number alone, so the file name and the part number are the same value; the H1 is the part number and a `- Name:` line in Source repeats the name) in the folder of BOM-041: `part def P_&lt;PN&gt;` with `partNumber` (always a string), `revision` (CL1+CL2 as exported) and `source` attributes. Lifecycle state is not written (BOM-021). The part name is the `description` YAML property. The parent's block owns one part usage per line: whole-number counts with unit ea/piece become multiplicity; any other quantity is `quantity` plus the standard unit (BOM-019); line, pinned `childRevision`, reference designator, effectivity dates, SSC and notes are attributes. Drawing lines (name starts DWG) become a `Document` note under `BOM/Drawings` (with `item def D_&lt;PN&gt;`) that the parent references with `ref item` (BOM-025); they are not part usages. Alternate-source rows attach to the line above and are written as BOM-028. | [BOM-017](70_Rule_References/BOM/BOM-001_020/BOM-017.md) — Active · P2 · live — The latest revision of a part number is the highest of the revisions it has as an assembly, or of those its parents pin when it is only a child (BOM-018); the part note lists the other revisions in the BOM. A parent pinning an older child revision gets a `flagConflict`; the child's body lists its older-revision users. Part numbers already noted in the vault are left as they are. Findings go to `99_System/11_Import/BOM Findings &lt;date&gt;.md`. EA to BOM matching by part number appearing in one EA element name, EA alias or PN tag is a report only. A part number that is already in the vault is kept; if the new file disagrees with it on any line (pinned revision, count or quantity, or a line that is new) the finding `EXISTING_DIFFERS` names every line that differs and nothing is rewritten. | [BOO-007](70_Rule_References/BOO/BOO-001_020/BOO-007.md) — Active · P1 — Bootstrap runs after the person trusts the vault, about two seconds after layout. It checks each locked plugin's files against the lock, enables those that match, merges the lock's governed settings into their `data.json` (the person's other keys stay), enables required and switches off prohibited core plugins, and reports what it did in one notice. A plugin whose hash differs or whose file is missing stays off and is named. It never downloads. The command "Check and repair this vault" runs it again. | [BOO-008](70_Rule_References/BOO/BOO-001_020/BOO-008.md) — Active · P1 — When `.vault.yaml` has no author, Bootstrap asks for a full name once, derives the author code, writes `author:` and creates the person note. Obsidian's "Trust author and enable plugins" choice is stored outside the vault and cannot be set by any file, so Bootstrap can only act after it is made. | [WKB-002](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-002.md) — Unreviewed · P1 — Workbench performance and cache architecture: one disposable semantic cache bound to vault_uid and schema versions; A/B commit slots; authored-link evidence for reconciliation; revision-scoped shared assurance service; ctime+mtime+size fingerprinting; path-hash buckets; core readiness before Local Model hydration; metadata-settled link re-resolution; bounded runtime history; a read failure is a scoped finding and never poisons the cache. | [WKB-003](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-003.md) — Unreviewed · P1 — (duplicate IDs in log) Workbench exposes one lightweight clickable runtime-health surface that never runs expensive work; live editor and metadata events are coalesced per path in a 250 ms quiet window. (REG-254, W-351b,W-352b) | [STK-00013 Engineering Knowledge Base](10_Stakeholder_Needs/Engineering%20Knowledge%20Base.md) — Shared product knowledge, structure, specifications and relationships. | [UC-00002 Search for a Part, Function or Design](20_Use_Cases/Search%20for%20a%20Part,%20Function%20or%20Design.md) — Search for a part, function, or design. | — | [BEH-00003 Discover and Resolve Model Elements](30_Functions/Discover%20and%20Resolve%20Model%20Elements.md) — Find notes by engineering name, alias, ID, SysML or metadata. | [OBJ-00003 MDSE Workbench](50_Architecture/MDSE%20Workbench/MDSE%20Workbench.md) — Obsidian tool for searching, viewing, reviewing and editing models. | — | Candidate; trace unvalidated |
| [WKB-004](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-004.md) — Unreviewed · P1 — Workbench runtime staging: bounded parallel shard reads; core model usable before Local Model hydration; heavy work staggered into non-overlapping lanes; elapsed-time work slices (12 ms) not fixed counts; background hydration is activity-aware, preemptible and promotable on demand; subsystems fail independently with separate health evidence; time-to-core-ready measured separately; cache persistence is single-flight and never starts hydration; views declare needsLocalOccurrences; no model I/O in onload or layout-ready. | [WKB-005](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-005.md) — Unreviewed · P3 — Workbench is an editor, not only a viewer, for Local Model context and reusable definitions through separate surfaces. Internal Structure view is a presentation of one Local Model context; Canvas geometry is never model semantics. | [WKB-006](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-006.md) — Unreviewed · P3 — Workbench has distinct structural and visual representations. A child usage is edited by locating its owner and changing the owning source region. Edits show a preview and semantic diff, check source hash for staleness, save atomically (or journal and recover) and re-index. Moving or renaming notes never changes structure. | [WKB-007](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-007.md) — Unreviewed · P3 — Graph staleness: edits are validated at commit. A graph opened against a newer model revision freezes interaction and prompts Reset/Refresh. Never overwrite the model from a stale graph or mutate SysML merely by opening a view. | [WKB-008](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-008.md) — Active · P1 — The Edit lens adds relationships, elements and requirement text. Relationship kinds offered depend on the selected note's SysML definition; targets come from a searchable, folder-grouped browser limited to eligible notes. Structure and links are written into the source note's `## SysML v2` block (D-0003); a new note gets type, subtype, id, uid, status and its block only. | [WKB-010](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-010.md) — Active · P1 — A new note's id is the class prefix plus the highest existing number plus one (MOD-007); its uid is local time plus the author code from `.vault.yaml` and moves forward one millisecond on a clash (MOD-004). | [WKB-012](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-012.md) — Active · P1 — Relationship properties are written inside the relationship in SysML: a `doc` comment, `attribute name = value;` (or `attribute name { doc … }` when the value is not a plain number or simple text), usage modifiers `ordered` and `nonunique`, quantity and direction. The drop-down offers only the options that relationship has; a free attribute name is accepted only where the relationship allows it (not specializes or verifies). Renaming a usage is refused when its name is used elsewhere in the block. | [WKB-014](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-014.md) — Active · P1 — The Edit lens has a Ports tab for a part, item, port or interface definition. It adds, changes and removes: port definition features (`in&#124;out&#124;inout item&#124;attribute&#124;port`), port usages (`[ref] port p : [~]PortDef [mult] [ordered] [nonunique]`), interface definition ends (`end e : [~]PortDef`), connections (`connect`, `connection [n] [: Def] connect`, `interface [n] [: Def] connect`, with optional `end ::&gt;` bindings), flows (`flow [n] [of Item] from a.x to b.y`) and bindings (`bind a.x = b.y`). Types are chosen in the searchable browser; paths are offered from the owner's parts and their ports. Every option has a "?". | [WKB-015](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-015.md) — Active · P1 — The Ports tab checks before it previews: names are unique in the owner, a port or end is typed by a port definition, only items and attributes have a direction, a path must resolve through the owner's usages (inherited ones included), an interface connection joins ports, each port matches its end's type and conjugation, and a flow starts at an out (or inout) feature and ends at an in (or inout) one, with every `~` on the path reversing directions. Type or end mismatches that SysML allows (bound features of different types, both ports unconjugated) are shown as notes, not refusals. A member that has a body (properties, description) is not rewritten by the Ports tab. | [WKB-018](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-018.md) — Active · P1 — A merge keeps the surviving note's file name, id, uid and SysML package. The removed note's content is folded in: frontmatter (more specific subtype, EA class, tags and relationship fields combined), user text under `## Merged from &lt;title&gt;`, Source lines combined without repeats, SysML members carried over with the removed definition's symbol rewritten to the survivor's and its specializations added. A member both notes define differently stays in the survivor and the other is kept under `## Not carried over from &lt;title&gt;` as sysml text. The removed title is listed under `## Aliases` and its id and uid under `## Former ids`. | [WKB-019](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-019.md) — Active · P1 — A merge rewrites every other note: `[[Removed]]` becomes `[[Kept&#124;Removed]]` in text (frontmatter lists get the plain new name, without repeats or links to itself) and the removed definition's symbol becomes the kept one in every SysML block. A note with more than one definition, a survivor with no SysML block and notes of different classes are refused with the reason. | [WKB-022](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-021_040/WKB-022.md) — Active · P1 — The Workbench keeps structure, design types, internal structure, function, states and requirements apart. Structure shows parts only. Design types shows only what a definition specializes and what specializes it. Internal shows the parts with the ports each part's type offers (inherited ones included), the definition's own ports, and every connection, binding and flow with both ends resolved; an end that names a part or port that does not exist is marked. Function shows actions and States shows states, each drawn alone. Requirements shows the trace around the selected note. The Obsidian panel has the same lenses. ## Coverage

- 386 rules; 341 requirement-note rows (332 original + nine atomic WKB-008); 159 verification-note rows; 227 rule-only gap rows; total **727**.
- **Additional candidates**: See [decomposition register](71_Rule_Analysis/DECOMPOSITION_REGISTER.csv); this readable primary-chain matrix does not silently promote multiple heuristic matches.
- No verification case is a passed test solely because the definition exists. Semantic approval, exact native SysML relationships and validated execution remain separate.
